<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove stable self-similar finite-time blowup for a supercritical defocusing nonlinear Schrödinger equation on the twelve-dimensional torus. For a sufficiently large odd power, the blowup initial data contain a nonempty open set in a high Sobolev space. As a consequence, Gaussian Fourier initial data of arbitrarily high Sobolev regularity can have positive probability of finite-time blowup.

- **claim-blowup-open-set** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-blowup)): There exist an odd integer $p\geq3$, a finite integer $k>8$, and a nonempty open set $\mathcal U\subset H^k(\mathbb T^{12};\mathbb C)$ with the following property. For every $u_0\in\mathcal U$, the unique local solution of <a href="#eq:nls" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:nls">[eq:nls]</a> extends to $$u\in C([0,T);H^k)\cap C^1([0,T);H^{k-2})$$ for some finite $T>0$, but has no continuous $H^k$ extension through $T$. More precisely, there are $x_*\in\mathbb T^{12}$ and $c_0>0$ such that $$\lim_{t\uparrow T}(T-t)^{1/(p-1)}\|u(t,x_*)\|=c_0.$$ The power can be chosen sufficiently large, and $k$ is then fixed after that choice.
  - Explanation: Shows that, for some large odd power and some high Sobolev index, a nonempty open set of initial data on the twelve-dimensional torus blows up in finite time. The blowup is self-similar, with a definite pointwise rate at one point.
- **claim-gaussian-blowup** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-gaussian)): For the fixed power $p$ and index $k$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:blowup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:blowup">1</a>, and for every $\alpha>k+6$, the initial datum $$\label{eq:gaussian} u_0^\omega(x)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z^{12}} g_n(\omega)\langle n\rangle^{-\alpha}e^{in\cdot x}$$ belongs to $H^k$ almost surely and has positive probability of finite-time blowup. In particular, the following assertion is false: for every energy-supercritical pair $(d,p)$ there is a finite $\alpha_0(d,p)$ such that, for every $\alpha>\alpha_0(d,p)$ and every $$d/2+2<m<\alpha-d/2,$$ the local $H^m$ solution with the Gaussian datum extends almost surely to $C(\mathbb R;H^m(\mathbb T^d))$.
  - Explanation: For the fixed power and index of the theorem, Gaussian Fourier data with fast enough decay is almost surely in the Sobolev space but blows up with positive probability. So the universal almost-sure global existence assertion is false.
- **claim-free-matching** (theorem, [source](#stmt-free-main)): For every $(b,Z)\in\mathcal D$, the following conclusions hold.* 1. *The quotient $$j(b,Z)=-\frac{H'(-ib,6,-iZ)}{H(-ib,6,-iZ)}$$ is well defined and satisfies $\|j(b,Z)\|<3\cdot10^{-9}$. It is nonzero on $\partial\mathcal D$, and, identifying $\mathbb C$ with $\mathbb R^2$ in the order real part, imaginary part, $$\deg(j,\mathcal D,0)=-1.$$* 2. *For every real $z\ge Z$, $H(-ib,6,-iz)\ne0$ and $$\label{free:exterior-bounds} \operatorname{Re}j(b,z)<.043.$$ For $z\ge3$ one also has $\operatorname{Im}j(b,z)>0$.* 3. *For $\ell\in\{0,1,2,\ldots\}$ and $h\in\{1,-1\}$ define $$\label{free:jet} q_h=\lambda+\ell/2-hi b,\qquad J_h(\lambda)= \begin{pmatrix}H(q_h,6+\ell,-hiZ)\\-H'(q_h,6+\ell,-hiZ)\end{pmatrix}.$$ The columns $J_h$ are holomorphic and nonzero on a neighborhood of $\operatorname{Re}\lambda\ge-1/32$. The determinant $\Delta_\ell(\lambda;b,Z)=\det(J_+(\lambda),J_-(\lambda))$ has no zero on $\operatorname{Re}\lambda=-1/32$. In the closed half-plane $\operatorname{Re}\lambda\ge-1/32$ its numbers of zeros, counted with holomorphic multiplicity, are $$\begin{array}{c\|ccc} \ell&0&1&\ell\ge2\\ \hline \#\{\Delta_\ell=0\}&2&1&0. \end{array}$$* *The locations of the symmetry zeros are identified later at parameters where $j(b,Z)=0$; the count in this proposition holds on the entire disk.
  - Explanation: Gives the free matching statements on the parameter disk: a bounded logarithmic-derivative quotient with nonzero degree, exterior sign bounds, and exact zero counts of the matching determinant for each angular degree.
- **claim-slow-solution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-free-slow)): For $\operatorname{Re}q>-1$, $\operatorname{Re}x\ge0$, $x\ne0$, define, with principal powers, $$\label{free:H-definition} H(q,m,x)=x^{-q}\left\{1+\frac1{\Gamma(q)} \int_0^\infty e^{-u}u^{q-1} \left[(1+u/x)^{m-1-q}-1\right]\,du\right\}.$$ Here $m$ may in particular be any positive integer used below. This definition is holomorphic in $q$, including $q=0$, and in $x$ on the interior of the indicated region, with holomorphic continuation across each nonzero point of its imaginary boundary. Primes on $H$ denote derivatives in its last argument. It satisfies $$\label{free:H-equation} xH''+(m-x)H'-qH=0,\qquad H'(q,m,x)=-qH(q+1,m+1,x),\qquad H(0,m,x)=1.$$ For every $N\ge1$ it has the expansion $$\label{free:H-expansion} H(q,m,x)=x^{-q}\left\{ \sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{(-1)^j(q)_j(1+q-m)_j}{j!x^j} +O(\|x\|^{-N})\right\}.$$ Here $(q)_0=1$ and $(q)_j=\prod_{n=0}^{j-1}(q+n)$ for $j\ge1$. The expansion is uniform for $\operatorname{Re}x\ge0$, $\|x\|\ge1$, and compact parameter sets. The same remainder estimate holds after any fixed number of $x\partial_x$ derivatives of the bracket. The symbol expansion specifies the slow solution, without an oscillatory component on the imaginary rays.
  - Explanation: Defines the regularized slow confluent hypergeometric solution by an integral, shows it is holomorphic including at zero first parameter, satisfies the ODE and derivative identity, and has a nonoscillatory asymptotic expansion.
- **claim-laguerre-recurrence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-free-laguerre)): Let $L_n(t)=e^t\partial_t^n(e^{-t}t^n)/n!$ be the Laguerre polynomials, orthonormal for $e^{-t}\,dt$ on $[0,\infty)$. Write $$G=\sum_{n\ge0}g_nL_n,\qquad B_n=\sum_{k\ge n}g_k,\qquad C_n=\sum_{k\ge n}(k-n)g_k.$$ These series and their parameter derivatives converge rapidly. With $t_n=q+n$ and $d_n=t_n-M$, one has $$\begin{aligned} &(B_0,C_0)=(G(0),-G'(0)),\label{free:initial-tail}\\ &t_ng_n=-MB_{n+1}-sC_n,\qquad d_nB_{n+1}=t_nB_n+sC_n,\qquad C_{n+1}=C_n-B_{n+1}.\label{free:recurrence} \end{aligned}$$ The real quadratic form $$\label{free:cone-form} p_n=\frac M2\|B_n\|^2+\operatorname{Re}(s\overline{B_n}C_n)$$ satisfies the exact identity $$\label{free:cone-increment} p_{n+1}-p_n= (\operatorname{Re}\lambda+n-5/2)\|g_n\|^2.$$
  - Explanation: Expands the slow solution in Laguerre polynomials, derives the coefficient and tail recurrences, and proves an exact increment identity for a real quadratic cone form.
- **claim-strict-cone** (theorem, [source](#stmt-free-cone)): Suppose $\operatorname{Re}\lambda\ge-1/32$ and $K\ge3$. For $n\ge K$ the normalized coefficients $\widehat g_n=g_n/(q)_K$, and their sums $\widehat B_K=B_K/(q)_K$, $\widehat C_K=C_K/(q)_K$, extend holomorphically across $q=0$. They satisfy $$\label{free:strict-cone} \frac M2\|\widehat B_K\|^2+ \operatorname{Re}(s\overline{\widehat B_K}\widehat C_K) =-\sum_{n\ge K}(\operatorname{Re}\lambda+n-5/2) \|\widehat g_n\|^2<0.$$ Consequently $\widehat C_K\ne0$, and $$\label{free:tail-disk} R_h=\widehat B_K/\widehat C_K\quad\hbox{is holomorphic},\qquad \|R_h+s/M\|<Z/M.$$
  - Explanation: The normalized Laguerre tail data extend across the removable singular parameter and satisfy a strictly negative cone inequality. Hence the tail ratio is holomorphic and lies in an explicit open disk.
- **claim-matching-columns** (theorem, [source](#stmt-free-columns)): Define $$\label{free:backward} F_n=\begin{pmatrix}d_n-s&-s\\t_n&t_n\end{pmatrix},\qquad P_K=F_0F_1\cdots F_{K-1}.$$ Then, including parameters where an individual $t_n$ or $d_n$ vanishes, $$\label{free:column-identity} P_K\begin{pmatrix}R_h\\1\end{pmatrix} =\frac1{\widehat C_K} \begin{pmatrix}H(q,M+1,-s)\\-H'(q,M+1,-s)\end{pmatrix}.$$ The scalar on the right is holomorphic and nonzero. The column is nonzero.
  - Explanation: A product of explicit matrices applied to the tail ratio reproduces the value-and-derivative column of the slow solution up to a nonzero holomorphic scalar. The column is nonzero.
- **claim-high-angular-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-free-high-angular)): For $(b,Z)\in\mathcal D$, $\ell\ge4$, and $\operatorname{Re}\lambda\ge-1/32$, one has $\Delta_\ell(\lambda;b,Z)\ne0$.
  - Explanation: The matching determinant has no zero in the closed half-plane for all high angular degrees. The proof uses a multiplier identity along conjugate complex paths.
- **claim-counting-boundary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-free-boundary)): There is a rectangle with left edge $\operatorname{Re}\lambda=-1/32$ and other edges sufficiently far to the right and above and below, uniformly for $(b,Z)\in\mathcal D$ and $0\le\rho\le1$, on whose boundary the homotoped determinant is nonzero. The same exclusions hold on every larger such rectangle.
  - Explanation: A fixed counting rectangle exists on whose boundary the homotoped determinant never vanishes, uniformly over the disk and the homotopy parameter, so zeros can be counted by the argument principle.
- **claim-winding-count** (theorem, [source](#stmt-free-winding)): For $0\le\ell\le3$ the zero counts in Proposition <a href="#free:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="free:main">3</a> are $2,1,0,0$, respectively.
  - Explanation: For the low angular degrees the number of determinant zeros in the half-plane is exactly the stated list, obtained from sign patterns of two axis polynomials and an argument-change count.
- **claim-profile-matching-degree** (theorem, [source](#stmt-free-profile-degree)): The first conclusion of Proposition <a href="#free:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="free:main">3</a> holds. More precisely there is an invertible real-linear map $\mathcal L:\mathbb R^2\to\mathbb C$ with negative determinant and minimum stretch greater than $.048$ such that $$\label{free:profile-linearization} \|j(b,Z)-\mathcal L(b-b_*,Z-Z_*)\|<2.5\cdot10^{-11} \qquad((b,Z)\in\mathcal D).$$ In particular $\|j\|>4.55\cdot10^{-10}$ on $\partial\mathcal D$.
  - Explanation: The quotient is approximated on the disk by an explicit invertible real-linear map of negative determinant with a guaranteed minimum stretch. This yields the boundary margin and the nonzero degree.
- **claim-exterior-inequalities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-free-exterior)): For $(b,Z)\in\mathcal D$, $H(-ib,6,-iz)$ is nonzero for all $z\ge Z$, and the inequalities in <a href="#free:exterior-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="free:exterior-bounds">[free:exterior-bounds]</a> and $\operatorname{Im}j(b,z)>0$ for $z\ge3$ hold. In radial variables, for any nonzero constant multiple $Q(r)$ of $H(-ib,6,-ir^2/4)$, $$\label{free:radial-quotients} \frac{A'}A=-\frac r2\operatorname{Im}j(b,r^2/4),\qquad \frac wr=\frac12+\operatorname{Re}j(b,r^2/4), \quad A=\|Q\|,\quad w=r/2+2\partial_r\arg Q.$$
  - Explanation: On the whole exterior ray the slow solution does not vanish and the real and imaginary parts of the quotient obey sign bounds. These translate into monotonic amplitude and positive velocity ratio in radial variables.
- **claim-profile-existence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-profile-existence)): There is $a_0>0$ such that, for every $0<a<a_0$, there are $(b_a,Z_a)\in\mathcal D$ and a smooth nonvanishing radial stationary solution $Q_a=A_ae^{i\phi_a}$ of <a href="#profile:similarity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="profile:similarity">[profile:similarity]</a>, normalized by $Q_a(0)>0$. They have the following properties.* 1. *The identities <a href="#profile:amplitude" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="profile:amplitude">[profile:amplitude]</a>–<a href="#profile:divergence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="profile:divergence">[profile:divergence]</a> hold, $0<P_a\le1$, and there are constants $c,C>0$, independent of small $a$, such that $$\label{profile:coercive-bounds} C^{-1}\langle r\rangle^{-4a}\le\mu_a(r) \le C\langle r\rangle^{-4a},\qquad \nabla w_a\ge c I.$$* 2. *If $a_n\downarrow0$ and $(b_{a_n},Z_{a_n})\to(b,Z)$, then $j(b,Z)=0$, where $j$ is the free matching function of Proposition <a href="#free:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="free:main">3</a>. With $R=2\sqrt Z$, the profiles converge in $C^1$ on every compact ball to $$\label{profile:limit} Q_0(r)= \begin{cases} 1,&0\le r\le R,\\[2pt] \displaystyle\frac{H(-ib,6,-ir^2/4)}{H(-ib,6,-iZ)},&r\ge R. \end{cases}$$ The exterior amplitude is strictly decreasing and has a positive limit at infinity. The convergence is $C^\infty$ on compact subsets of $\{r>R\}$. On every fixed ball, $$\label{profile:pressure-limit} P_{a_n}\ \stackrel{*}{\rightharpoonup}\ b\mathbf1_{\{r<R\}} \quad\hbox{in }L^\infty,$$ and $\mu_{a_n},w_{a_n}$ converge in $C^1$. In particular $P_{a_n}/a_n\to0$ locally uniformly on $\{r>R\}$.* 3. *On the fixed exterior $r\ge r_b$, $$\label{profile:tail} Q_a(r)=r^{\nu_a}f_a(r),\qquad \nu_a=-2a+2ib_a, \qquad 0<\kappa\le\|f_a(r)\|\le\rho<1,$$ with $\kappa,\rho$ independent of $a$. There are coefficients $m_{a,j}$, with $\|m_{a,0}\|\ge\kappa$, such that for every pair of fixed nonnegative integers $N,J$, $$\label{profile:symbol-expansion} \left\|D^j\left(f_a(r)-\sum_{q=0}^{N}m_{a,q}r^{-2q}\right)\right\| \le C_{N,J}r^{-2N-2},\qquad 0\le j\le J,\quad r\ge r_b.$$ The constants are uniform as $a\downarrow0$. In particular, $$\label{profile:exterior-pressure} \|D^j(P_a/a)\|\le\eta_j(a)r^{-2},\qquad \|D^je^{2i\phi_a}\|\le C_j,\qquad \eta_j(a)\longrightarrow0$$ for each fixed $j$. On the inner ball, $$\label{profile:pressure-transport} W_a(P_a/a)\le Ca(P_a/a),\qquad r\le r_b,$$ whereas $\|W_a(P_a/a)\|\le\eta(a)r^{-2}$ for $r\ge r_b$, with $\eta(a)\to0$. Consequently the positive part of $W_a(P_a/a)$ is bounded uniformly on the whole space.* 4. *For each fixed $a$, every Cartesian derivative obeys $$\label{profile:fixed-power-symbols} \|\partial^\beta Q_a(y)\|\le C_{a,\beta}\langle y\rangle^{-2a-\|\beta\|}, \qquad \left\|\partial^\beta\big((P_a/a)e^{2i\phi_a}\big)\right\| +\|\partial^\beta(P_a/a)\| \le C_{a,\beta}\langle y\rangle^{-2-\|\beta\|}.$$ The time-symmetry combination has the improved exterior order $$\label{profile:time-symbol} \left\|D^j\left((a-ib_a+D/2)Q_a\right)\right\| \le C_j r^{-2a-2},\qquad r\ge r_b.$$
  - Explanation: For all small enough parameter a, smooth nonvanishing radial stationary profiles exist with matched parameters in the disk, uniform coercive bounds, a core limit, exterior symbol expansions, and pressure transport estimates.
- **claim-boundary-modulus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-profile-boundary-modulus)): Uniformly on $\mathcal D$, $$\label{profile:boundary-gap} 1-.2\varepsilon^2<h_b<1-.15\varepsilon^2.$$ For fixed $b$ in the projection of $\mathcal D$, the function $R'\mapsto\|F(r_b;b,R')\|$ is strictly increasing on $[r_0,r_b)$.
  - Explanation: The modulus of the free inner solution at the fixed boundary radius lies in a narrow band just below one, and it increases strictly with the starting radius.
- **claim-inner-amplitude** (theorem, [source](#stmt-profile-inner)): For all sufficiently small $a>0$ and all $(b,Z)\in\mathcal D$, the system <a href="#profile:amplitude" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="profile:amplitude">[profile:amplitude]</a> has a positive radial solution on $[0,r_b]$ with $A'(0)=0$ and $A(r_b)=h_b$. Within the bounds $$\label{profile:inner-bounds} h_b\le A\le .5^a,$$ it is unique. It depends continuously on $(b,Z)$, and obeys $$\label{profile:bulk-barrier} A(r)\ge .2^a-.02(r-r_0)_+^2.$$ The amplitudes are bounded uniformly in $C^{1,1}[0,r_b]$.
  - Explanation: The inner amplitude problem has a positive radial solution with the prescribed boundary value, unique within explicit bounds, continuous in the parameters, with a lower barrier and uniform regularity.
- **claim-core-identification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-profile-core-limit)): For the inner solutions of Lemma <a href="#profile:inner" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="profile:inner">15</a>, suppose $a_n\downarrow0$ and $(b_n,Z_n)\to(b,Z)\in\mathcal D$. Then their complex reconstructions converge in $C^1[0,r_b]$ to the function equal to $1$ on $[0,R(Z)]$ and to $F_{\rm in}$ on $[R(Z),r_b]$. Their pressures converge weak star to $b\mathbf1_{\{r<R(Z)\}}$. These convergence assertions are uniform over the compact parameter disk in the sequential sense just stated.
  - Explanation: As the small parameter tends to zero the inner solutions converge to a constant core joined to the free solution, and the pressures converge weak star to a step supported in the core.
- **claim-slow-exterior** (theorem, [source](#stmt-profile-exterior)): For all sufficiently small $a>0$ and $(b,Z)\in\mathcal D$, there is a nonvanishing exterior stationary solution $Q^{\rm out}_a$ with $Q^{\rm out}_a(r_b)=h_b$. It depends continuously on $(a,b,Z)$, down to the limit <a href="#profile:normalized-free" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="profile:normalized-free">[profile:normalized-free]</a>, with convergence in every fixed derivative on compact exterior intervals. Its factor $f=r^{2a-2ib}Q^{\rm out}_a$ satisfies the bounds and expansions in <a href="#profile:tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="profile:tail">[profile:tail]</a>–<a href="#profile:symbol-expansion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="profile:symbol-expansion">[profile:symbol-expansion]</a>, uniformly over the entire parameter disk.
  - Explanation: A nonvanishing exterior stationary solution with the prescribed boundary value exists, depends continuously on the parameters down to the free limit, and has uniform bounds and symbol expansions.
- **claim-degree-matching** (theorem, [source](#stmt-profile-matching)): For every sufficiently small $a>0$ there is $(b_a,Z_a)\in\mathcal D$ at which the inner and outer profiles have the same logarithmic derivative at $r_b$. After a constant phase rotation of the outer profile they form a smooth nonvanishing stationary profile. Every parameter limit as $a\downarrow0$ is a zero of $j$ in $\mathcal D$.
  - Explanation: A degree argument gives parameters at which inner and outer logarithmic derivatives agree, producing a smooth nonvanishing profile; every parameter limit is a zero of the free quotient.
- **claim-pressure-deformation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-profile-pressure)): The matched profiles satisfy <a href="#profile:coercive-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="profile:coercive-bounds">[profile:coercive-bounds]</a> and <a href="#profile:exterior-pressure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="profile:exterior-pressure">[profile:exterior-pressure]</a>–<a href="#profile:pressure-transport" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="profile:pressure-transport">[profile:pressure-transport]</a>.
  - Explanation: The matched profiles obey the coercive bounds, exterior pressure decay and the pressure transport inequality, including positive deformation of the velocity field.
- **claim-mode-classification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spectrum-classification)): For all sufficiently small admissible $a>0$, the only regular outgoing modes with $\operatorname{Re}\lambda\ge-1/32$ are the phase, translation, and time modes. Their first circular components and eigenvalues are $$iQ\quad(\lambda=0),\qquad \partial_jQ\quad(\lambda=1/2, 1\le j\le12),\qquad (a-ib+D/2)Q\quad(\lambda=1).$$ The second component of each listed real symmetry generator is the conjugate of the displayed component. Their complex spans are the corresponding complexified eigenspaces. In each fixed spherical harmonic, each eigenvalue that occurs is algebraically simple; degrees $\ell\ge2$ have no such eigenvalue.* *Here “outgoing” means that, in each component, for every fixed integer $j\ge0$, $$\label{spec:slow-condition} D^jv_h=O(r^{-2(a+\operatorname{Re}\lambda)})\qquad(r\longrightarrow\infty).$$ The same classification holds for regular solutions for which $\partial_r^kv_h\in L^2(r^{11}\,dr)$ for some integer $k\ge7$. There is no regular generalized mode above any of these eigenvectors with this top-derivative integrability. Algebraic simplicity for outgoing modes means simplicity of the analytic regular/outgoing boundary problem, including its differentiated outgoing condition.
  - Explanation: In the half-plane of interest the only regular outgoing modes are the phase, translation and time modes, each algebraically simple in its harmonic, and no higher angular degree contributes.
- **claim-block-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-block-reduction)): Suppose that $$DY=(r^2\Lambda(r)+B(r))Y$$ on a half-line, where $\Lambda$ is diagonal, its selected blocks have uniformly separated spectra, and $\Lambda,B$ and their fixed Euler derivatives are bounded. For every fixed $m$ there is an invertible change $I+O_m(r^{-2})$, with the same symbol bounds, after which the off-block remainder is $O_m(r^{-m})$. The bounded part in each diagonal block changes by $O_m(r^{-2})$. The changes preserve holomorphic dependence on any parameters on which the original system depends holomorphically.
  - Explanation: A system dominated by a diagonal quadratic term with separated blocks can be reduced by near-identity changes so the off-block remainder is as small as desired, preserving holomorphic dependence.
- **claim-slow-basis** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-slow-basis)): For bounded $\lambda,\ell$ and small $a$, the exterior solution space of <a href="#spec:circular" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:circular">[spec:circular]</a> has a two-dimensional outgoing subspace, locally holomorphic in $\lambda$. Its bases and parameter derivatives converge locally on $r\ge r_b$ to free outgoing bases as $a\to0$. Every homogeneous solution satisfying the top-derivative condition in Theorem <a href="#spectrum:classification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spectrum:classification">20</a> belongs to this subspace.
  - Explanation: The exterior solution space contains a two-dimensional outgoing subspace depending holomorphically on the spectral parameter, converging to free bases, and containing every solution with the top-derivative integrability.
- **claim-real-part-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-real-bound)): For all sufficiently small $a$, there is no nonzero regular outgoing mode with $\operatorname{Re}\lambda\ge4$.
  - Explanation: No nonzero regular outgoing mode exists with large real part, by a weighted energy identity with a Hardy inequality.
- **claim-remote-robin** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-remote-data)): Every outgoing solution along this sequence satisfies $$\label{spec:remote-robin} q_h'(E)-hi\sqrt{F_h(E)}q_h(E)=O(E^{-1})\|q(E)\|.$$ The constant is uniform after discarding finitely many sequence terms.
  - Explanation: At a remote radius outgoing solutions satisfy a Robin-type relation between the value and derivative with a small error.
- **claim-scalar-turning-point** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-scalar-turn)): Fix one channel and write $F=F_h$. For $R\in[B_0,B_1]$, let $U$ solve $$U''+(F+hi\gamma)U=0,\qquad U(E)=F(E)^{-1/4},\quad U'(E)=hi\sqrt{F(E)}U(E).$$ If $F$ has a zero $r_0\in[R,E]$, define $$\label{spec:turn-scales} \begin{split} A_0&=r_0^2/16,\qquad H_0=L_\ell/r_0^2,\qquad k_0^2=A_0+H_0,\\ d_0&=(r_0/(2k_0^2))^{1/3},\qquad K(r)=(\|F(r)\|+d_0^{-2})^{1/2},\\ H_f(r)&=\int_{\min(r,r_0)}^{r_0}\sqrt{-F(t)}\,dt. \end{split}$$ If there is no zero, put $K=\sqrt F$ and $H_f=0$. For $N_r(u)=K(r)^{1/2}\|u(r)\|+K(r)^{-1/2}\|u'(r)\|$, after taking a subsequence common to the two channels, there are $c,C>0$ such that $$\begin{aligned} N_r(u)&\le C e^{\|H_f(r)-H_f(t)\|}N_t(u),\label{spec:scalar-transfer}\\ \|U(R)\|&\ge cK(R)^{-1/2}e^{H_f(R)},\label{spec:scalar-value}\\ U'(R)/U(R)&=\mathcal B_h(R)+o(n_*),\label{spec:scalar-slope} \end{aligned}$$ where $$\mathcal B_h(R)= \begin{cases}-\sqrt{-F_h(R)},&F_h(R)<0,\\ hi\sqrt{F_h(R)},&F_h(R)>0. \end{cases}$$ The first estimate holds for every scalar homogeneous solution and all $r,t\in[R,E]$. All left endpoint statements are uniform for $R\in[B_0,B_1]$.
  - Explanation: For the scalar equation with at most one turning point, a transfer bound, a lower bound on the value and the logarithmic slope at the shell are established using a WKB analysis and an Airy limit.
- **claim-shell-robin** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-boundary-estimate)): After subselection, every outgoing solution along an escaping parameter sequence satisfies, uniformly on $B_0\le R\le B_1$, $$\label{spec:shell-robin} q_h'(R)=\mathcal B_h(R)q_h(R)+o(n_*)\|q(R)\|.$$ The error is an operator bound on the two-dimensional outgoing space.
  - Explanation: Along escaping parameter sequences, outgoing solutions satisfy coupled Robin data on a fixed shell with a small operator error.
- **claim-escaping-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-high-exclusion)): There is no sequence of nonzero regular outgoing modes with $a\to0$, $-1/32\le\operatorname{Re}\lambda\le4$, and $\ell+\|\operatorname{Im}\lambda\|\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: No sequence of nonzero regular outgoing modes can escape to large angular or imaginary parameters; the two cases are ruled out by energy and flux identities.
- **claim-inverse-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-inverse-limit)): Fix $\ell$ and a convergent profile subsequence. Let $R=2\sqrt Z$ be its limiting core radius, and fix a sufficiently large outer radius $B_2>r_b$. Let $E$ be the Hilbert space of $H^1$ pairs in this harmonic on the ball $r<B_2$, and let $$\mathscr Z=L^2(r<B_2;\mathbb C^2)\oplus\mathbb C^2,\qquad j_E X=(X,X(B_2)).$$ The map $j_E:E\to\mathscr Z$ is compact. Define $T_a:E'\to E$ by the coercive form $$\label{spec:base-form} \mathfrak b_a(X,\Theta)= \int\left(\nabla\Theta^*\nabla X+\Theta^*X +(P/a)\overline{\Theta_f}f\right)\mu.$$ The dual $E'$ consists of conjugate-linear functionals on test vectors. Let $$E_0=\{(f,g)\in E:f=0\text{ a.e. on }r<R\},$$ and let $T_0:E'\to E_0$ be the inverse of the limiting form, with no pressure term and tests restricted to $E_0$. Then $$\label{spec:inverse-norm} \\|j_ET_a-j_ET_0\\|_{E'\to\mathscr Z}\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Inverses of the coercive forms with singular pressure converge, after composition with a compact map, to the inverse of the constrained limiting form.
- **claim-compact-pencil** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-compact-pencil)): Near each bounded candidate $\lambda$, choose $B_2$ sufficiently large. The regular outgoing mode equation is equivalent to $$\label{spec:compact-equation} (I-\mathcal C_a(\lambda))z=0\quad\hbox{on }\mathscr Z,\qquad \mathcal C_a(\lambda)=j_ET_aK_a(\lambda).$$ These are compact holomorphic families, and $\mathcal C_a\to\mathcal C_0$ locally uniformly in operator norm.
  - Explanation: The regular outgoing mode equation is equivalent to a kernel problem for compact holomorphic operators that converge locally uniformly in operator norm.
- **claim-interface-conditions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-interface)): The limiting kernel in <a href="#spec:compact-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:compact-equation">[spec:compact-equation]</a> consists of free slow exterior modes satisfying $$\label{spec:matching} f(R)=0,\qquad g(R+)=g(R-),\qquad g'(R+)=g'(R-),\qquad g(r)=c r^\ell\quad(r<R).$$ It is nonzero exactly when the two-column determinant of Proposition <a href="#free:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="free:main">3</a> vanishes.
  - Explanation: The limiting kernel consists of free slow exterior modes satisfying interface matching conditions, and it is nonzero exactly when the two-column determinant vanishes.
- **claim-symmetry-roots** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-simplicity)): At the limiting disk root $j(b,Z)=0$, the only roots of <a href="#spec:free-determinant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:free-determinant">[spec:free-determinant]</a> in $\operatorname{Re}\lambda\ge-1/32$ are $0,1$ for $\ell=0$ and $1/2$ for $\ell=1$, each simple. The compact pencil $I-\mathcal C_0$ has a one-dimensional kernel at each such root and no first-order analytic chain.
  - Explanation: At the limiting root of the free quotient the determinant roots in the half-plane are exactly the symmetry eigenvalues, each simple, and the limiting pencil has no first-order chain.
- **claim-spatial-estimates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lin-spatial)): The embeddings $Y\hookrightarrow C_0(\mathbb R^{12})$ and $Y_L\hookrightarrow L^\infty(\mathbb T_L^{12})$ are bounded, uniformly for $L\ge1$. Both spaces are algebras, with a uniform algebra constant for $Y_L$. For a ball or annulus of radius $R\ge1$, contained in one torus cell when appropriate and with $R\le C_0L$, one has $$\label{lin:local-derivatives} \\|\nabla^jv\\|_{L^2(\text{ball or annulus})} \le C R^{\max(s_0-j,0)}\\|v\\|, \qquad 0\le j\le k.$$ Here and below an undecorated norm denotes the applicable $Y$ or $Y_L$ norm, and constants may depend on the fixed geometric constant $C_0$.* *If $F$ satisfies $\|\nabla^jF\|\le C_j\langle y\rangle^{-\beta-j}$, then a smooth dyadic piece supported where $\|y\|\asymp R$ satisfies $$\label{lin:symbol-norm} \\|\psi_RF\\|\le C\big(R^{6-\beta-s_0}+R^{6-\beta-k}\big),$$ also on the tori for $R\le C_0L$. In particular $Q\in Y$, and for every fixed $\chi\in C_c^\infty(B_1)$ the cutoff profiles $\chi(y/L)Q(y)$ have uniformly bounded $Y_L$ norms.
  - Explanation: The scale-adapted spaces embed boundedly into bounded functions uniformly in the torus scale, are algebras, and satisfy local derivative bounds and symbol norm bounds.
- **claim-compact-errors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lin-compact-error)): For every $\varepsilon>0$ there are $R_\varepsilon,C_\varepsilon<\infty$ such that $$\label{lin:compact-bound} \\|\mathcal V_*v\\|_{s_0,*} +\\|\nabla^k(\mathcal V_*v)-\mathcal V_*\nabla^kv\\|_2 \le\varepsilon\\|v\\|+C_\varepsilon\\|v\\|_{L^2(B_{R_\varepsilon})}.$$ Here $*$ denotes either the whole-space potential and homogeneous low-order norm, or a torus potential at its current scale and the regularized low-order norm in <a href="#lin:YL" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="lin:YL">[lin:YL]</a>. The constants are uniform for all sufficiently large current torus scales. The potential acts componentwise on the ordered tensor in the second term.
  - Explanation: The potential terms and commutators are small in the full norm up to a localized low-order term, with constants uniform for large torus scales.
- **claim-energy-observation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lin-observation)): There are $c>0$, $C<\infty$, and $R_0<\infty$ such that every whole-space linear solution, and every torus linear solution with sufficiently large starting scale, satisfies $$\label{lin:energy-observation} \frac{d}{ds}\\|v(s)\\|^2 \le-c\\|v(s)\\|^2+C\\|v(s)\\|_{L^2(B_{R_0})}^2.$$ The derivative can equivalently be understood through the integrated inequality. The constants are independent of the torus scale and of the slab length. In particular, for $t\ge0$, $$\label{lin:integrated-observation} \\|v(t)\\|^2\le e^{-ct}\\|v(0)\\|^2 +C\int_0^t e^{-c(t-s)}\\|v(s)\\|_{L^2(B_{R_0})}^2\,ds.$$
  - Explanation: The linear energy decays exponentially up to a localized observation term, with constants independent of torus scale and slab length.
- **claim-stable-evolution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-linear-decay)): There is a bounded real-linear map $\pi:Y\to\mathbb R^{14}$ such that $$\label{lin:spectral-coordinates} \pi E=I,\qquad S(s)E=Ee^{s\Lambda},\qquad \pi S(s)=e^{s\Lambda}\pi\quad(s\in\mathbb R).$$ For some $C_S<\infty$ and $\eta>0$, $$\label{lin:stable-decay} \\|S(s)v\\|_Y\le C_Se^{-\eta s}\\|v\\|_Y, \qquad s\ge0,\quad \pi v=0.$$ Thus $E\pi$ is the symmetry spectral projection. Constants depend on the fixed profile, $a$, and $k$.
  - Explanation: There is a bounded spectral projection onto the symmetry directions that intertwines the evolution with a matrix exponential, and the evolution decays exponentially on its kernel.
- **claim-torus-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-linear-torus)): For each fixed $M>0$ the step maps $\mathcal F_L^{(M)}$ are uniformly bounded for all sufficiently large $L$. Writing $L'=Le^{M/2}$, $$\label{lin:coordinate-defect} \lim_{L\to\infty} \\|\pi_{L'}\mathcal F_L^{(M)}-e^{M\Lambda}\pi_L\\| _{\mathcal L(Y_L,\mathbb R^{14})}=0.$$ There exists $M_0<\infty$ such that for every fixed $M\ge M_0$ there is $L_0(M)<\infty$ for which $$\label{lin:torus-stable-contraction} \\|(I-P_{L'})\mathcal F_L^{(M)}\|_{\ker\pi_L}\\| _{\mathcal L(Y_L,Y_{L'})}\le\frac18, \qquad L\ge L_0(M).$$ Moreover $\\|(I-P_{L'})\mathcal F_L^{(M)}E_L\\|\le C_M$. Any prescribed positive bound on the coordinate defect can be imposed by enlarging $L_0(M)$ after $M$ has been fixed. These bounds persist after restarting a step at any larger scale.* *After identifying torus spaces by dilation, the maps above and the step propagator are continuous when the scale and the input vary continuously. This last assertion is strong continuity for varying inputs, and does not assert operator-norm continuity of Schrödinger time evolution.
  - Explanation: Step maps on expanding tori are uniformly bounded, converge in coordinates to the whole-space flow, and contract on the stable subspace after a long enough step.
- **claim-nonlinear-step** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nl-step)): There are $\delta>0$, $L_0<\infty$ and $C_M<\infty$ such that, for $L\geq L_0$, the perturbation equation $U=Q_s+v$ has a unique solution through the step whenever $\\|v(0)\\|_{Y_L}\leq\delta$. Its endpoint is $$v(M)=\mathcal F_Lv(0)+h_L(v(0)),$$ where $$\begin{aligned} \\|h_L(0)\\|&\leq C_M L^{-2-a},\label{nl:step-zero}\\ \operatorname{Lip}_{\\|v\\|\leq d}h_L &\leq C_M(d+L^{-2-a})\qquad(0<d\leq\delta). \label{nl:step-lip} \end{aligned}$$ During the step, $$\sup_{0\leq s\leq M}\\|v(s)\\|_{Y_{L_s}} \leq C_M\bigl(\\|v(0)\\|_{Y_L}+L^{-2-a}\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Over one step the nonlinear perturbation equation has a unique solution that equals the linear evolution plus a correction with small size and Lipschitz constant.
- **claim-stable-graph** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nl-graph)): For all sufficiently large $L$ there is a continuous map $G_L$, defined on a fixed small ball in $\ker\pi_L$, such that the initial perturbation $$v_0=w_0+E_LG_L(w_0)$$ generates a solution on every similarity-time slab and $$\\|v(s)\\|_{Y_{L_s}}\longrightarrow0\quad(s\to\infty).$$ Uniformly in $L$, $$\label{nl:graph-size} \|G_L(w_0)\|\leq C\bigl(\\|w_0\\|+L^{-2-a}\bigr).$$ The coordinate $G_L(w_0)$ is jointly continuous in $L$ and $w_0$ after identifying periodic functions by dilation.
  - Explanation: A continuous graph over the stable subspace gives initial perturbations whose solutions decay in similarity time, with a size bound uniform in the scale.
- **claim-parameter-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nl-parameter-expansion)): For $\|d'\|\leq\rho$ with $\rho$ fixed and small, uniformly as $T_0\to0$, $$\label{nl:leading-expansion} v_0(d';u_{\mathrm{ref}}) =E_L C d'+r(d'),\qquad C d'=(-\theta+b\beta,\zeta,\beta),$$ and $$\\|r(d')\\|_{Y_L}\leq C\bigl(\|d'\|^2+\sqrt{T_0}\|d'\|\bigr).$$ For fixed $T_0$, replacing $u_{\mathrm{ref}}$ by $u_{\mathrm{in}}$ adds at most $C(T_0)\\|u_{\mathrm{in}}-u_{\mathrm{ref}}\\|_{H^k}$, uniformly on the parameter ball. All these maps are continuous.
  - Explanation: Varying phase, center and blowup time moves the starting perturbation along the symmetry directions through an invertible matrix, up to a quadratic remainder and a small localization error.

        "exact_quote": "For every $(b,Z)\\in\\mathcal D$, the following conclusions hold.*\n\n1.  *The quotient $$j(b,Z)=-\\frac{H'(-ib,6,-iZ)}{H(-ib,6,-iZ)}$$ is well defined and satisfies $|j(b,Z)|<3\\cdot10^{-9}$.         "anchor": "stmt-free-slow",
        "exact_quote": "For $\\operatorname{Re}q>-1$, $\\operatorname{Re}x\\ge0$, $x\\ne0$, define, with principal powers, $$\\label{free:H-definition}\n H(q,m,x)=x^{-q}\\left\\{1+\\frac1{\\Gamma(q)}\n \\int_0^\\infty e^{-u}u^{q-1}\n \\left[(1+u/x)^{m-1-q}-1\\right]\\,du\\right\\}.$$ Here $m$ may in particular be any positive integer used below. This definition is holomorphic in $q$, including $q=0$, and in $x$ on the interior of the indicated region, with holomorphic continuation across each nonzero point of its imaginary boundary. Primes on $H$ denote derivatives in its last argument. It satisfies $$\\label{free:H-equation}\n xH''+(m-x)H'-qH=0,\\qquad\n H'(q,m,x)=-qH(q+1,m+1,x),\\qquad H(0,m,x)=1.$$ For every $N\\ge1$ it has the expansion $$\\label{free:H-expansion}\n H(q,m,x)=x^{-q}\\left\\{\n \\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\\frac{(-1)^j(q)_j(1+q-m)_j}{j!x^j}\n +O(|x|^{-N})\\right\\}.$$ Here $(q)_0=1$ and $(q)_j=\\prod_{n=0}^{j-1}(q+n)$ for $j\\ge1$. The expansion is uniform for $\\operatorname{Re}x\\ge0$, $|x|\\ge1$, and compact parameter sets. The same remainder estimate holds after any fixed number of $x\\partial_x$ derivatives of the bracket. The symbol expansion specifies the slow solution, without an oscillatory component on the imaginary rays.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $L_n(t)=e^t\\partial_t^n(e^{-t}t^n)/n!$ be the Laguerre polynomials, orthonormal for $e^{-t}\\,dt$ on $[0,\\infty)$. Write $$G=\\sum_{n\\ge0}g_nL_n,\\qquad\n B_n=\\sum_{k\\ge n}g_k,\\qquad C_n=\\sum_{k\\ge n}(k-n)g_k.$$ These series and their parameter derivatives converge rapidly. With $t_n=q+n$ and $d_n=t_n-M$, one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n &(B_0,C_0)=(G(0),-G'(0)),\\label{free:initial-tail}\\\\\n &t_ng_n=-MB_{n+1}-sC_n,\\qquad\n d_nB_{n+1}=t_nB_n+sC_n,\\qquad\n C_{n+1}=C_n-B_{n+1}.\\label{free:recurrence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The real quadratic form $$\\label{free:cone-form}\n p_n=\\frac M2|B_n|^2+\\operatorname{Re}(s\\overline{B_n}C_n)$$ satisfies the exact identity $$\\label{free:cone-increment}\n p_{n+1}-p_n=\n (\\operatorname{Re}\\lambda+n-5/2)|g_n|^2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-371.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-free-cone",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\operatorname{Re}\\lambda\\ge-1/32$ and $K\\ge3$. For $n\\ge K$ the normalized coefficients $\\widehat g_n=g_n/(q)_K$, and their sums $\\widehat B_K=B_K/(q)_K$, $\\widehat C_K=C_K/(q)_K$, extend holomorphically across $q=0$. They satisfy $$\\label{free:strict-cone}\n \\frac M2|\\widehat B_K|^2+\n \\operatorname{Re}(s\\overline{\\widehat B_K}\\widehat C_K)\n =-\\sum_{n\\ge K}(\\operatorname{Re}\\lambda+n-5/2)\n |\\widehat g_n|^2<0.$$ Consequently $\\widehat C_K\\ne0$, and $$\\label{free:tail-disk}\n R_h=\\widehat B_K/\\widehat C_K\\quad\\hbox{is holomorphic},\\qquad\n |R_h+s/M|<Z/M.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-371.1"

        "exact_quote": "The first conclusion of Proposition <a href=\"#free:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"free:main\">3</a> holds.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-free-exterior",
        "exact_quote": "For $(b,Z)\\in\\mathcal D$, $H(-ib,6,-iz)$ is nonzero for all $z\\ge Z$, and the inequalities in <a href=\"#free:exterior-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"free:exterior-bounds\">[free:exterior-bounds]</a> and $\\operatorname{Im}j(b,z)>0$ for $z\\ge3$ hold. In radial variables, for any nonzero constant multiple $Q(r)$ of $H(-ib,6,-ir^2/4)$, $$\\label{free:radial-quotients}\n \\frac{A'}A=-\\frac r2\\operatorname{Im}j(b,r^2/4),\\qquad\n \\frac wr=\\frac12+\\operatorname{Re}j(b,r^2/4),\n \\quad A=|Q|,\\quad w=r/2+2\\partial_r\\arg Q.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-371.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-free-exterior",
          "exact_quote": "For $(b,Z)\\in\\mathcal D$, $H(-ib,6,-iz)$ is nonzero for all $z\\ge Z$, and the inequalities in <a href=\"#free:exterior-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"free:exterior-bounds\">[free:exterior-bounds]</a> and $\\operatorname{Im}j(b,z)>0$ for $z\\ge3$ hold. In radial variables, for any nonzero constant multiple $Q(r)$ of $H(-ib,6,-ir^2/4)$, $$\\label{free:radial-quotients}\n \\frac{A'}A=-\\frac r2\\operatorname{Im}j(b,r^2/4),\\qquad\n \\frac wr=\\frac12+\\operatorname{Re}j(b,r^2/4),\n \\quad A=|Q|,\\quad w=r/2+2\\partial_r\\arg Q.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-371.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $(b,Z)\\in\\mathcal D$, $H(-ib,6,-iz)$ is nonzero for all $z\\ge Z$, and the inequalities in <a href=\"#free:exterior-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"free:exterior-bounds\">[free:exterior-bounds]</a> and $\\operatorname{Im}j(b,z)>0$ for $z\\ge3$ hold. In radial variables, for any nonzero constant multiple $Q(r)$ of $H(-ib,6,-ir^2/4)$, $$\\label{free:radial-quotients}\n \\frac{A'}A=-\\frac r2\\operatorname{Im}j(b,r^2/4),\\qquad\n \\frac wr=\\frac12+\\operatorname{Re}j(b,r^2/4),\n \\quad A=|Q|,\\quad w=r/2+2\\partial_r\\arg Q.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-profile-existence",
        "exact_quote": "There is $a_0>0$ such that, for every $0<a<a_0$, there are $(b_a,Z_a)\\in\\mathcal D$ and a smooth nonvanishing radial stationary solution $Q_a=A_ae^{i\\phi_a}$ of <a href=\"#profile:similarity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"profile:similarity\">[profile:similarity]</a>, normalized by $Q_a(0)>0$. They have the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *The identities <a href=\"#profile:amplitude\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"profile:amplitude\">[profile:amplitude]</a>–<a href=\"#profile:divergence\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"profile:divergence\">[profile:divergence]</a> hold, $0<P_a\\le1$, and there are constants $c,C>0$, independent of small $a$, such that $$\\label{profile:coercive-bounds}\n     C^{-1}\\langle r\\rangle^{-4a}\\le\\mu_a(r)\n           \\le C\\langle r\\rangle^{-4a},\\qquad\n     \\nabla w_a\\ge c I.$$*\n\n\n2.  *If $a_n\\downarrow0$ and $(b_{a_n},Z_{a_n})\\to(b,Z)$, then $j(b,Z)=0$, where $j$ is the free matching function of Proposition <a href=\"#free:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"free:main\">3</a>. With $R=2\\sqrt Z$, the profiles converge in $C^1$ on every compact ball to $$\\label{profile:limit}\n     Q_0(r)=\n     \\begin{cases}\n     1,&0\\le r\\le R,\\\\[2pt]\n     \\displaystyle\\frac{H(-ib,6,-ir^2/4)}{H(-ib,6,-iZ)},&r\\ge R.\n     \\end{cases}$$ The exterior amplitude is strictly decreasing and has a positive limit at infinity. The convergence is $C^\\infty$ on compact subsets of $\\{r>R\\}$. On every fixed ball, $$\\label{profile:pressure-limit}\n     P_{a_n}\\ \\stackrel{*}{\\rightharpoonup}\\ b\\mathbf1_{\\{r<R\\}}\n           \\quad\\hbox{in }L^\\infty,$$ and $\\mu_{a_n},w_{a_n}$ converge in $C^1$. In particular $P_{a_n}/a_n\\to0$ locally uniformly on $\\{r>R\\}$.*\n\n\n\n\n\n3.  *On the fixed exterior $r\\ge r_b$, $$\\label{profile:tail}\n     Q_a(r)=r^{\\nu_a}f_a(r),\\qquad \\nu_a=-2a+2ib_a,\n     \\qquad 0<\\kappa\\le|f_a(r)|\\le\\rho<1,$$ with $\\kappa,\\rho$ independent of $a$. There are coefficients $m_{a,j}$, with $|m_{a,0}|\\ge\\kappa$, such that for every pair of fixed nonnegative integers $N,J$, $$\\label{profile:symbol-expansion}\n     \\left|D^j\\left(f_a(r)-\\sum_{q=0}^{N}m_{a,q}r^{-2q}\\right)\\right|\n           \\le C_{N,J}r^{-2N-2},\\qquad 0\\le j\\le J,\\quad r\\ge r_b.$$ The constants are uniform as $a\\downarrow0$. In particular, $$\\label{profile:exterior-pressure}\n     |D^j(P_a/a)|\\le\\eta_j(a)r^{-2},\\qquad\n     |D^je^{2i\\phi_a}|\\le C_j,\\qquad \\eta_j(a)\\longrightarrow0$$ for each fixed $j$. On the inner ball, $$\\label{profile:pressure-transport}\n     W_a(P_a/a)\\le Ca(P_a/a),\\qquad r\\le r_b,$$ whereas $|W_a(P_a/a)|\\le\\eta(a)r^{-2}$ for $r\\ge r_b$, with $\\eta(a)\\to0$. Consequently the positive part of $W_a(P_a/a)$ is bounded uniformly on the whole space.*\n\n\n\n4.  *For each fixed $a$, every Cartesian derivative obeys $$\\label{profile:fixed-power-symbols}\n     |\\partial^\\beta Q_a(y)|\\le C_{a,\\beta}\\langle y\\rangle^{-2a-|\\beta|},\n     \\qquad\n     \\left|\\partial^\\beta\\big((P_a/a)e^{2i\\phi_a}\\big)\\right|\n           +|\\partial^\\beta(P_a/a)|\n           \\le C_{a,\\beta}\\langle y\\rangle^{-2-|\\beta|}.$$ The time-symmetry combination has the improved exterior order $$\\label{profile:time-symbol}\n     \\left|D^j\\left((a-ib_a+D/2)Q_a\\right)\\right|\n           \\le C_j r^{-2a-2},\\qquad r\\ge r_b.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-371.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-profile-existence",
          "exact_quote": "There is $a_0>0$ such that, for every $0<a<a_0$, there are $(b_a,Z_a)\\in\\mathcal D$ and a smooth nonvanishing radial stationary solution $Q_a=A_ae^{i\\phi_a}$ of <a href=\"#profile:similarity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"profile:similarity\">[profile:similarity]</a>, normalized by $Q_a(0)>0$. They have the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *The identities <a href=\"#profile:amplitude\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"profile:amplitude\">[profile:amplitude]</a>–<a href=\"#profile:divergence\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"profile:divergence\">[profile:divergence]</a> hold, $0<P_a\\le1$, and there are constants $c,C>0$, independent of small $a$, such that $$\\label{profile:coercive-bounds}\n     C^{-1}\\langle r\\rangle^{-4a}\\le\\mu_a(r)\n           \\le C\\langle r\\rangle^{-4a},\\qquad\n     \\nabla w_a\\ge c I.$$*\n\n\n2.  *If $a_n\\downarrow0$ and $(b_{a_n},Z_{a_n})\\to(b,Z)$, then $j(b,Z)=0$, where $j$ is the free matching function of Proposition <a href=\"#free:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"free:main\">3</a>. With $R=2\\sqrt Z$, the profiles converge in $C^1$ on every compact ball to $$\\label{profile:limit}\n     Q_0(r)=\n     \\begin{cases}\n     1,&0\\le r\\le R,\\\\[2pt]\n     \\displaystyle\\frac{H(-ib,6,-ir^2/4)}{H(-ib,6,-iZ)},&r\\ge R.\n     \\end{cases}$$ The exterior amplitude is strictly decreasing and has a positive limit at infinity. The convergence is $C^\\infty$ on compact subsets of $\\{r>R\\}$. On every fixed ball, $$\\label{profile:pressure-limit}\n     P_{a_n}\\ \\stackrel{*}{\\rightharpoonup}\\ b\\mathbf1_{\\{r<R\\}}\n           \\quad\\hbox{in }L^\\infty,$$ and $\\mu_{a_n},w_{a_n}$ converge in $C^1$. In particular $P_{a_n}/a_n\\to0$ locally uniformly on $\\{r>R\\}$.*\n\n\n\n\n\n3.  *On the fixed exterior $r\\ge r_b$, $$\\label{profile:tail}\n     Q_a(r)=r^{\\nu_a}f_a(r),\\qquad \\nu_a=-2a+2ib_a,\n     \\qquad 0<\\kappa\\le|f_a(r)|\\le\\rho<1,$$ with $\\kappa,\\rho$ independent of $a$. There are coefficients $m_{a,j}$, with $|m_{a,0}|\\ge\\kappa$, such that for every pair of fixed nonnegative integers $N,J$, $$\\label{profile:symbol-expansion}\n     \\left|D^j\\left(f_a(r)-\\sum_{q=0}^{N}m_{a,q}r^{-2q}\\right)\\right|\n           \\le C_{N,J}r^{-2N-2},\\qquad 0\\le j\\le J,\\quad r\\ge r_b.$$ The constants are uniform as $a\\downarrow0$. In particular, $$\\label{profile:exterior-pressure}\n     |D^j(P_a/a)|\\le\\eta_j(a)r^{-2},\\qquad\n     |D^je^{2i\\phi_a}|\\le C_j,\\qquad \\eta_j(a)\\longrightarrow0$$ for each fixed $j$. On the inner ball, $$\\label{profile:pressure-transport}\n     W_a(P_a/a)\\le Ca(P_a/a),\\qquad r\\le r_b,$$ whereas $|W_a(P_a/a)|\\le\\eta(a)r^{-2}$ for $r\\ge r_b$, with $\\eta(a)\\to0$. Consequently the positive part of $W_a(P_a/a)$ is bounded uniformly on the whole space.*\n\n\n\n4.  *For each fixed $a$, every Cartesian derivative obeys $$\\label{profile:fixed-power-symbols}\n     |\\partial^\\beta Q_a(y)|\\le C_{a,\\beta}\\langle y\\rangle^{-2a-|\\beta|},\n     \\qquad\n     \\left|\\partial^\\beta\\big((P_a/a)e^{2i\\phi_a}\\big)\\right|\n           +|\\partial^\\beta(P_a/a)|\n           \\le C_{a,\\beta}\\langle y\\rangle^{-2-|\\beta|}.$$ The time-symmetry combination has the improved exterior order $$\\label{profile:time-symbol}\n     \\left|D^j\\left((a-ib_a+D/2)Q_a\\right)\\right|\n           \\le C_j r^{-2a-2},\\qquad r\\ge r_b.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-371.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is $a_0>0$ such that, for every $0<a<a_0$, there are $(b_a,Z_a)\\in\\mathcal D$ and a smooth nonvanishing radial stationary solution $Q_a=A_ae^{i\\phi_a}$ of <a href=\"#profile:similarity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"profile:similarity\">[profile:similarity]</a>, normalized by $Q_a(0)>0$. They have the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *The identities <a href=\"#profile:amplitude\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"profile:amplitude\">[profile:amplitude]</a>–<a href=\"#profile:divergence\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"profile:divergence\">[profile:divergence]</a> hold, $0<P_a\\le1$, and there are constants $c,C>0$, independent of small $a$, such that $$\\label{profile:coercive-bounds}\n     C^{-1}\\langle r\\rangle^{-4a}\\le\\mu_a(r)\n           \\le C\\langle r\\rangle^{-4a},\\qquad\n     \\nabla w_a\\ge c I.$$*\n\n\n2.  *If $a_n\\downarrow0$ and $(b_{a_n},Z_{a_n})\\to(b,Z)$, then $j(b,Z)=0$, where $j$ is the free matching function of Proposition <a href=\"#free:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"free:main\">3</a>. With $R=2\\sqrt Z$, the profiles converge in $C^1$ on every compact ball to $$\\label{profile:limit}\n     Q_0(r)=\n     \\begin{cases}\n     1,&0\\le r\\le R,\\\\[2pt]\n     \\displaystyle\\frac{H(-ib,6,-ir^2/4)}{H(-ib,6,-iZ)},&r\\ge R.\n     \\end{cases}$$ The exterior amplitude is strictly decreasing and has a positive limit at infinity. The convergence is $C^\\infty$ on compact subsets of $\\{r>R\\}$. On every fixed ball, $$\\label{profile:pressure-limit}\n     P_{a_n}\\ \\stackrel{*}{\\rightharpoonup}\\ b\\mathbf1_{\\{r<R\\}}\n           \\quad\\hbox{in }L^\\infty,$$ and $\\mu_{a_n},w_{a_n}$ converge in $C^1$. In particular $P_{a_n}/a_n\\to0$ locally uniformly on $\\{r>R\\}$.*\n\n\n\n\n\n3.  *On the fixed exterior $r\\ge r_b$, $$\\label{profile:tail}\n     Q_a(r)=r^{\\nu_a}f_a(r),\\qquad \\nu_a=-2a+2ib_a,\n     \\qquad 0<\\kappa\\le|f_a(r)|\\le\\rho<1,$$ with $\\kappa,\\rho$ independent of $a$. There are coefficients $m_{a,j}$, with $|m_{a,0}|\\ge\\kappa$, such that for every pair of fixed nonnegative integers $N,J$, $$\\label{profile:symbol-expansion}\n     \\left|D^j\\left(f_a(r)-\\sum_{q=0}^{N}m_{a,q}r^{-2q}\\right)\\right|\n           \\le C_{N,J}r^{-2N-2},\\qquad 0\\le j\\le J,\\quad r\\ge r_b.$$ The constants are uniform as $a\\downarrow0$. In particular, $$\\label{profile:exterior-pressure}\n     |D^j(P_a/a)|\\le\\eta_j(a)r^{-2},\\qquad\n     |D^je^{2i\\phi_a}|\\le C_j,\\qquad \\eta_j(a)\\longrightarrow0$$ for each fixed $j$. On the inner ball, $$\\label{profile:pressure-transport}\n     W_a(P_a/a)\\le Ca(P_a/a),\\qquad r\\le r_b,$$ whereas $|W_a(P_a/a)|\\le\\eta(a)r^{-2}$ for $r\\ge r_b$, with $\\eta(a)\\to0$. Consequently the positive part of $W_a(P_a/a)$ is bounded uniformly on the whole space.*\n\n\n\n4.  *For each fixed $a$, every Cartesian derivative obeys $$\\label{profile:fixed-power-symbols}\n     |\\partial^\\beta Q_a(y)|\\le C_{a,\\beta}\\langle y\\rangle^{-2a-|\\beta|},\n     \\qquad\n     \\left|\\partial^\\beta\\big((P_a/a)e^{2i\\phi_a}\\big)\\right|\n           +|\\partial^\\beta(P_a/a)|\n           \\le C_{a,\\beta}\\langle y\\rangle^{-2-|\\beta|}.$$ The time-symmetry combination has the improved exterior order $$\\label{profile:time-symbol}\n     \\left|D^j\\left((a-ib_a+D/2)Q_a\\right)\\right|\n           \\le C_j r^{-2a-2},\\qquad r\\ge r_b.$$",
      "explanation": "For all small enough parameter a, smooth nonvanishing radial stationary profiles exist with matched parameters in the disk, uniform coercive bounds, a core limit, exterior symbol expansions, and pressure transport estimates.",

        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently small $a>0$ and all $(b,Z)\\in\\mathcal D$, the system <a href=\"#profile:amplitude\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"profile:amplitude\">[profile:amplitude]</a> has a positive radial solution on $[0,r_b]$ with $A'(0)=0$ and $A(r_b)=h_b$.         "anchor": "stmt-spectrum-classification",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently small admissible $a>0$, the only regular outgoing modes with $\\operatorname{Re}\\lambda\\ge-1/32$ are the phase, translation, and time modes. Their first circular components and eigenvalues are $$iQ\\quad(\\lambda=0),\\qquad\n \\partial_jQ\\quad(\\lambda=1/2, 1\\le j\\le12),\\qquad\n (a-ib+D/2)Q\\quad(\\lambda=1).$$ The second component of each listed real symmetry generator is the conjugate of the displayed component. Their complex spans are the corresponding complexified eigenspaces. In each fixed spherical harmonic, each eigenvalue that occurs is algebraically simple; degrees $\\ell\\ge2$ have no such eigenvalue.*\n\n\n*Here “outgoing” means that, in each component, for every fixed integer $j\\ge0$, $$\\label{spec:slow-condition}\n D^jv_h=O(r^{-2(a+\\operatorname{Re}\\lambda)})\\qquad(r\\longrightarrow\\infty).$$ The same classification holds for regular solutions for which $\\partial_r^kv_h\\in L^2(r^{11}\\,dr)$ for some integer $k\\ge7$. There is no regular generalized mode above any of these eigenvectors with this top-derivative integrability. Algebraic simplicity for outgoing modes means simplicity of the analytic regular/outgoing boundary problem, including its differentiated outgoing condition.",
        "source_id": "PM-371.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

Their complex spans are the corresponding complexified eigenspaces. In each fixed spherical harmonic, each eigenvalue that occurs is algebraically simple; degrees $\\ell\\ge2$ have no such eigenvalue.*\n\n\n*Here “outgoing” means that, in each component, for every fixed integer $j\\ge0$, $$\\label{spec:slow-condition}\n D^jv_h=O(r^{-2(a+\\operatorname{Re}\\lambda)})\\qquad(r\\longrightarrow\\infty).$$ The same classification holds for regular solutions for which $\\partial_r^kv_h\\in L^2(r^{11}\\,dr)$ for some integer $k\\ge7$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-block-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $$DY=(r^2\\Lambda(r)+B(r))Y$$ on a half-line, where $\\Lambda$ is diagonal, its selected blocks have uniformly separated spectra, and $\\Lambda,B$ and their fixed Euler derivatives are bounded. For every fixed $m$ there is an invertible change $I+O_m(r^{-2})$, with the same symbol bounds, after which the off-block remainder is $O_m(r^{-m})$.       },

      "exact_statement": "Suppose that $$DY=(r^2\\Lambda(r)+B(r))Y$$ on a half-line, where $\\Lambda$ is diagonal, its selected blocks have uniformly separated spectra, and $\\Lambda,B$ and their fixed Euler derivatives are bounded. For every fixed $m$ there is an invertible change $I+O_m(r^{-2})$, with the same symbol bounds, after which the off-block remainder is $O_m(r^{-m})$. The bounded part in each diagonal block changes by $O_m(r^{-2})$. The changes preserve holomorphic dependence on any parameters on which the original system depends holomorphically.",
      "explanation": "A system dominated by a diagonal quadratic term with separated blocks can be reduced by near-identity changes so the off-block remainder is as small as desired, preserving holomorphic dependence.",

      "hypotheses": [

        "anchor": "stmt-spec-scalar-turn",
        "exact_quote": "Fix one channel and write $F=F_h$. For $R\\in[B_0,B_1]$, let $U$ solve $$U''+(F+hi\\gamma)U=0,\\qquad\n U(E)=F(E)^{-1/4},\\quad U'(E)=hi\\sqrt{F(E)}U(E).$$ If $F$ has a zero $r_0\\in[R,E]$, define $$\\label{spec:turn-scales}\n \\begin{split}\n A_0&=r_0^2/16,\\qquad H_0=L_\\ell/r_0^2,\\qquad k_0^2=A_0+H_0,\\\\\n d_0&=(r_0/(2k_0^2))^{1/3},\\qquad\n K(r)=(|F(r)|+d_0^{-2})^{1/2},\\\\\n H_f(r)&=\\int_{\\min(r,r_0)}^{r_0}\\sqrt{-F(t)}\\,dt.\n \\end{split}$$ If there is no zero, put $K=\\sqrt F$ and $H_f=0$. For $N_r(u)=K(r)^{1/2}|u(r)|+K(r)^{-1/2}|u'(r)|$, after taking a subsequence common to the two channels, there are $c,C>0$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n N_r(u)&\\le C e^{|H_f(r)-H_f(t)|}N_t(u),\\label{spec:scalar-transfer}\\\\\n |U(R)|&\\ge cK(R)^{-1/2}e^{H_f(R)},\\label{spec:scalar-value}\\\\\n U'(R)/U(R)&=\\mathcal B_h(R)+o(n_*),\\label{spec:scalar-slope}\n \n\\end{aligned}$$ where $$\\mathcal B_h(R)=\n \\begin{cases}-\\sqrt{-F_h(R)},&F_h(R)<0,\\\\\n hi\\sqrt{F_h(R)},&F_h(R)>0.\n \\end{cases}$$ The first estimate holds for every scalar homogeneous solution and all $r,t\\in[R,E]$. All left endpoint statements are uniform for $R\\in[B_0,B_1]$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-spec-inverse-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\ell$ and a convergent profile subsequence. Let $R=2\\sqrt Z$ be its limiting core radius, and fix a sufficiently large outer radius $B_2>r_b$. Let $E$ be the Hilbert space of $H^1$ pairs in this harmonic on the ball $r<B_2$, and let $$\\mathscr Z=L^2(r<B_2;\\mathbb C^2)\\oplus\\mathbb C^2,\\qquad\n j_E X=(X,X(B_2)).$$ The map $j_E:E\\to\\mathscr Z$ is compact. Define $T_a:E'\\to E$ by the coercive form $$\\label{spec:base-form}\n \\mathfrak b_a(X,\\Theta)=\n \\int\\left(\\nabla\\Theta^*\\nabla X+\\Theta^*X\n                         +(P/a)\\overline{\\Theta_f}f\\right)\\mu.$$ The dual $E'$ consists of conjugate-linear functionals on test vectors. Let $$E_0=\\{(f,g)\\in E:f=0\\text{ a.e. on }r<R\\},$$ and let $T_0:E'\\to E_0$ be the inverse of the limiting form, with no pressure term and tests restricted to $E_0$. Then $$\\label{spec:inverse-norm}\n \\|j_ET_a-j_ET_0\\|_{E'\\to\\mathscr Z}\\longrightarrow0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-371.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-inverse-limit",

Define $T_a:E'\\to E$ by the coercive form $$\\label{spec:base-form}\n \\mathfrak b_a(X,\\Theta)=\n \\int\\left(\\nabla\\Theta^*\\nabla X+\\Theta^*X\n                         +(P/a)\\overline{\\Theta_f}f\\right)\\mu.$$ The dual $E'$ consists of conjugate-linear functionals on test vectors. Let $$E_0=\\{(f,g)\\in E:f=0\\text{ a.e. on }r<R\\},$$ and let $T_0:E'\\to E_0$ be the inverse of the limiting form, with no pressure term and tests restricted to $E_0$. Then $$\\label{spec:inverse-norm}\n \\|j_ET_a-j_ET_0\\|_{E'\\to\\mathscr Z}\\longrightarrow0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-371.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-spec-simplicity",
        "exact_quote": "At the limiting disk root $j(b,Z)=0$, the only roots of <a href=\"#spec:free-determinant\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:free-determinant\">[spec:free-determinant]</a> in $\\operatorname{Re}\\lambda\\ge-1/32$ are $0,1$ for $\\ell=0$ and $1/2$ for $\\ell=1$, each simple.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lin-spatial",
        "exact_quote": "The embeddings $Y\\hookrightarrow C_0(\\mathbb R^{12})$ and $Y_L\\hookrightarrow L^\\infty(\\mathbb T_L^{12})$ are bounded, uniformly for $L\\ge1$. Both spaces are algebras, with a uniform algebra constant for $Y_L$. For a ball or annulus of radius $R\\ge1$, contained in one torus cell when appropriate and with $R\\le C_0L$, one has $$\\label{lin:local-derivatives}\n \\|\\nabla^jv\\|_{L^2(\\text{ball or annulus})}\n \\le C R^{\\max(s_0-j,0)}\\|v\\|,\n \\qquad 0\\le j\\le k.$$ Here and below an undecorated norm denotes the applicable $Y$ or $Y_L$ norm, and constants may depend on the fixed geometric constant $C_0$.*\n\n\n*If $F$ satisfies $|\\nabla^jF|\\le C_j\\langle y\\rangle^{-\\beta-j}$, then a smooth dyadic piece supported where $|y|\\asymp R$ satisfies $$\\label{lin:symbol-norm}\n \\|\\psi_RF\\|\\le C\\big(R^{6-\\beta-s_0}+R^{6-\\beta-k}\\big),$$ also on the tori for $R\\le C_0L$. In particular $Q\\in Y$, and for every fixed $\\chi\\in C_c^\\infty(B_1)$ the cutoff profiles $\\chi(y/L)Q(y)$ have uniformly bounded $Y_L$ norms.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lin-compact-error",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\varepsilon>0$ there are $R_\\varepsilon,C_\\varepsilon<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{lin:compact-bound}\n \\|\\mathcal V_*v\\|_{s_0,*}\n +\\|\\nabla^k(\\mathcal V_*v)-\\mathcal V_*\\nabla^kv\\|_2\n \\le\\varepsilon\\|v\\|+C_\\varepsilon\\|v\\|_{L^2(B_{R_\\varepsilon})}.$$ Here $*$ denotes either the whole-space potential and homogeneous low-order norm, or a torus potential at its current scale and the regularized low-order norm in <a href=\"#lin:YL\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"lin:YL\">[lin:YL]</a>.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lin-observation",
        "exact_quote": "There are $c>0$, $C<\\infty$, and $R_0<\\infty$ such that every whole-space linear solution, and every torus linear solution with sufficiently large starting scale, satisfies $$\\label{lin:energy-observation}\n \\frac{d}{ds}\\|v(s)\\|^2\n \\le-c\\|v(s)\\|^2+C\\|v(s)\\|_{L^2(B_{R_0})}^2.$$ The derivative can equivalently be understood through the integrated inequality. The constants are independent of the torus scale and of the slab length. In particular, for $t\\ge0$, $$\\label{lin:integrated-observation}\n \\|v(t)\\|^2\\le e^{-ct}\\|v(0)\\|^2\n   +C\\int_0^t e^{-c(t-s)}\\|v(s)\\|_{L^2(B_{R_0})}^2\\,ds.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-371.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-linear-torus",
        "exact_quote": "For each fixed $M>0$ the step maps $\\mathcal F_L^{(M)}$ are uniformly bounded for all sufficiently large $L$. Writing $L'=Le^{M/2}$, $$\\label{lin:coordinate-defect}\n \\lim_{L\\to\\infty}\n \\|\\pi_{L'}\\mathcal F_L^{(M)}-e^{M\\Lambda}\\pi_L\\|\n _{\\mathcal L(Y_L,\\mathbb R^{14})}=0.$$ There exists $M_0<\\infty$ such that for every fixed $M\\ge M_0$ there is $L_0(M)<\\infty$ for which $$\\label{lin:torus-stable-contraction}\n \\|(I-P_{L'})\\mathcal F_L^{(M)}|_{\\ker\\pi_L}\\|\n       _{\\mathcal L(Y_L,Y_{L'})}\\le\\frac18,\n \\qquad L\\ge L_0(M).$$ Moreover $\\|(I-P_{L'})\\mathcal F_L^{(M)}E_L\\|\\le C_M$. Any prescribed positive bound on the coordinate defect can be imposed by enlarging $L_0(M)$ after $M$ has been fixed. These bounds persist after restarting a step at any larger scale.*\n\n*After identifying torus spaces by dilation, the maps above and the step propagator are continuous when the scale and the input vary continuously. This last assertion is strong continuity for varying inputs, and does not assert operator-norm continuity of Schrödinger time evolution.",
        "source_id": "PM-371.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nl-step",
        "exact_quote": "There are $\\delta>0$, $L_0<\\infty$ and $C_M<\\infty$ such that, for $L\\geq L_0$, the perturbation equation $U=Q_s+v$ has a unique solution through the step whenever $\\|v(0)\\|_{Y_L}\\leq\\delta$. Its endpoint is $$v(M)=\\mathcal F_Lv(0)+h_L(v(0)),$$ where $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|h_L(0)\\|&\\leq C_M L^{-2-a},\\label{nl:step-zero}\\\\\n \\operatorname{Lip}_{\\|v\\|\\leq d}h_L\n   &\\leq C_M(d+L^{-2-a})\\qquad(0<d\\leq\\delta).\n \\label{nl:step-lip}\n\\end{aligned}$$ During the step, $$\\sup_{0\\leq s\\leq M}\\|v(s)\\|_{Y_{L_s}}\n \\leq C_M\\bigl(\\|v(0)\\|_{Y_L}+L^{-2-a}\\bigr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-371.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $L$ there is a continuous map $G_L$, defined on a fixed small ball in $\\ker\\pi_L$, such that the initial perturbation $$v_0=w_0+E_LG_L(w_0)$$ generates a solution on every similarity-time slab and $$\\|v(s)\\|_{Y_{L_s}}\\longrightarrow0\\quad(s\\to\\infty).$$ Uniformly in $L$, $$\\label{nl:graph-size}\n |G_L(w_0)|\\leq C\\bigl(\\|w_0\\|+L^{-2-a}\\bigr).$$ The coordinate $G_L(w_0)$ is jointly continuous in $L$ and $w_0$ after identifying periodic functions by dilation.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **scaling-critical Sobolev exponent** ([source](#eq:nls)): Its scaling-critical Sobolev exponent is $s_{\mathrm{cr}}=d/2-2/(p-1)$.
- **small parameter a** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Write $a=1/(p-1)$.
- **construction parameters** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Throughout the construction $d=12$, $a>0$ is small, and $D=y\cdot\nabla$.
- **radial angular eigenvalue** ([source](#free:radial-equation)): Here $\ell(\ell+10)$ is the eigenvalue of the negative spherical Laplacian on degree-$\ell$ harmonics on $\mathbb S^{11}$.
- **limiting core radius** ([source](#free:disk)): The radius of the limiting core is $R=2\sqrt Z$.
- **Brouwer degree on the disk** ([source](#free:disk)): The degree on $\mathcal D$ means Brouwer degree on its interior.
- **Laguerre polynomials** ([source](#stmt-free-laguerre)): Let $L_n(t)=e^t\partial_t^n(e^{-t}t^n)/n!$ be the Laguerre polynomials, orthonormal for $e^{-t}\,dt$ on $[0,\infty)$.
- **Pochhammer symbol** ([source](#stmt-free-slow)): Here $(q)_0=1$ and $(q)_j=\prod_{n=0}^{j-1}(q+n)$ for $j\ge1$.
- **radial stationary profile** ([source](#pm-h-12)): We seek a nonvanishing radial stationary solution $Q=Ae^{i\phi}$, with $A>0$ and $\phi(0)=0$.
- **velocity vector field W** ([source](#profile:notation)): The same letter $w$ denotes the vector field $w(r)y/r$, extended smoothly at zero, and $W=w\cdot\nabla$.
- **low-order Sobolev exponent** ([source](#lin:Y)): Set $s_0=6-a$.
- **expanding torus** ([source](#lin:YL)): For $L\ge1$ put $\mathbb T_L^{12}=\mathbb R^{12}/(2\pi L\mathbb Z)^{12}$.
- **torus norm space Y_L** ([source](#lin:YL)): Thus $Y_L$ is $H^k(\mathbb T_L^{12})$ with a scale-dependent norm.
- **cutoff symmetry map** ([source](#lin:piL)): Define the cellwise cutoff symmetry map $E_L=\chi(y/L)E$.
- **step endpoint scale** ([source](#nl:step-condition)): For a step beginning at scale $L$, put $L'=Le^{M/2}$ and denote its linear evolution by $\mathcal F_L$.
- **coefficient enclosure** ([source](#cert:product-rule)): For a complex polynomial $X$, write $\|X\|_1$ for the polynomial whose $j$th coefficient is $\|\operatorname{Re}X_j\|+\|\operatorname{Im}X_j\|$.

| q-torus-contraction | stable contraction factor of a torus step | ≤ | $\frac18$ | long enough step and large enough scale |
| q-step-condition | step length condition | < | $1$ | sufficiently large step length |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-slow-solution** [claim](#stmt-free-slow) / [proof](#proof-stmt-free-slow): Regularized integral, integration by parts and splitting the integral for the expansion -> holomorphic slow solution with ODE, derivative identity and expansion
- **step-laguerre** [claim](#stmt-free-laguerre) / [proof](#proof-stmt-free-laguerre): Laguerre expansion, generating function and algebraic identity for the quadratic form -> coefficient recurrences and cone increment identity
- **step-cone** [claim](#stmt-free-cone) / [proof](#proof-stmt-free-cone): Normalize by the Pochhammer factor, sum the increment identity, complete the square -> strict cone inequality and disk for the tail ratio
- **step-columns** [claim](#stmt-free-columns) / [proof](#proof-stmt-free-columns): Division-free matrix recurrence and analytic continuation -> matching columns expressed through backward matrix product
- **step-high-angular** [claim](#stmt-free-high-angular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-free-high-angular): Multiplier identity along conjugate paths with a certified polynomial positivity check -> no zero for high angular degrees
- **step-counting-boundary** [claim](#stmt-free-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-free-boundary): Cone form on right and horizontal edges, positive-definite Hermitian form on the left edge -> nonvanishing determinant on a counting rectangle
- **step-winding** [claim](#stmt-free-winding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-free-winding): Homotopy of the tail ratio, argument principle and sign tables of axis polynomials -> zero counts for low angular degrees
- **step-profile-degree** [claim](#stmt-free-profile-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-free-profile-degree): Exact rational enclosures of a matrix product and straight-line homotopy to a linear map -> quotient approximated by an invertible linear map, degree minus one
- **step-exterior** [claim](#stmt-free-exterior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-free-exterior): Forward matrix and Hermitian form with exact coefficient checks -> exterior nonvanishing and sign bounds
- **step-free-matching** [claim](#stmt-free-main) / [proof](#stmt-free-main): Combine the high-angular, winding, degree and exterior lemmas -> free matching proposition
- **step-boundary-modulus** [claim](#stmt-profile-boundary-modulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-boundary-modulus): Bootstrap on the free inner ODE -> modulus band and monotonicity in the starting radius
- **step-inner-amplitude** [claim](#stmt-profile-inner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-inner): Monotone iteration with barriers, comparison principle and fixed point -> unique inner amplitude with bounds
- **step-core-limit** [claim](#stmt-profile-core-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-core-limit): Compactness and identification of the plateau -> limit profile and weak star pressure limit
- **step-slow-exterior** [claim](#stmt-profile-exterior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-exterior): Formal expansion and contraction in logarithmic radius, then continuation inward -> nonvanishing exterior solution with symbol expansion
- **step-degree-matching** [claim](#stmt-profile-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-matching): Wronskian identity and degree preserved under uniform convergence -> matched smooth profile
- **step-pressure-deformation** [claim](#stmt-profile-pressure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-pressure): Maximum principle for the derivative, exterior Euler estimates, limiting deformation bounds -> coercive and pressure transport bounds
- **step-profile-existence** [claim](#stmt-profile-existence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-profile-pressure-2): Collect the construction with the order of choices fixed -> profile family with uniform estimates
- **step-block-reduction** [claim](#stmt-spec-block-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-block-reduction): Successive commutator changes of variables -> block-reduced system
- **step-slow-basis** [claim](#stmt-spec-slow-basis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-slow-basis): Block reduction, integral equation from infinity, top-derivative integrability -> holomorphic two-dimensional outgoing subspace
- **step-real-bound** [claim](#stmt-spec-real-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-real-bound): Weighted energy identity and Hardy inequality -> no modes with large real part
- **step-remote-data** [claim](#stmt-spec-remote-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-remote-data): Diagonalization, block reduction and propagator bounds by Gronwall -> Robin relation at the remote endpoint
- **step-scalar-turn** [claim](#stmt-spec-scalar-turn) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-scalar-turn): WKB transfer, Airy window and growing-solution comparison -> transfer bound, value bound and slope at the shell
- **step-shell-robin** [claim](#stmt-spec-boundary-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-boundary-estimate): Green kernel bounds and variation of constants -> coupled Robin data on the shell
- **step-escaping-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-spec-high-exclusion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-high-exclusion): Energy identity in one case; flux identity, compactness and multiplier in the other -> no escaping mode sequences
- **step-inverse-limit** [claim](#stmt-spec-inverse-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-inverse-limit): Coercivity, weak limits and density of test functions -> convergence of constrained inverses
- **step-compact-pencil** [claim](#stmt-spec-compact-pencil) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-compact-pencil): Recast the mode equation with the outgoing condition as a compact operator equation -> compact holomorphic pencil converging in operator norm
- **step-interface** [claim](#stmt-spec-interface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-interface): Identify interface conditions from the form domain and reduce to the free determinant -> kernel characterized by the determinant
- **step-symmetry-roots** [claim](#stmt-spec-simplicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-simplicity): Symmetry identities, zero counts and exclusion of a first-order chain -> simple symmetry roots
- **step-mode-classification** [claim](#stmt-spectrum-classification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-simplicity-2): Argument principle for the pencil and contradiction by subsequence extraction -> classification of regular outgoing modes
- **step-spatial** [claim](#stmt-lin-spatial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lin-spatial): Fourier integrability, interpolation and dyadic localization -> uniform embeddings, algebra and local bounds
- **step-compact-errors** [claim](#stmt-lin-compact-error) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lin-compact-error): Dyadic tails, fractional interpolation and local compactness -> errors small up to a localized term
- **step-energy-observation** [claim](#stmt-lin-observation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lin-observation): Free norm rates plus compact error and a large derivative order -> energy inequality with localized observation
- **step-stable-evolution** [claim](#stmt-linear-decay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-linear-decay): Essential contraction of the time-one map, finite rank perturbation and contour bounds -> spectral projection and exponential decay on its kernel
- **step-torus-transfer** [claim](#stmt-linear-torus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-linear-torus): Compactness and uniqueness for local limits, then observation inequality before the limit -> uniform torus step bounds and contraction
- **step-nonlinear-step** [claim](#stmt-nl-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nl-step): Contraction on short substeps and Gronwall -> unique step solution with Lipschitz correction
- **step-stable-graph** [claim](#stmt-nl-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nl-graph): Discrete Lyapunov-Perron contraction in weighted sequence norm -> continuous graph of decaying perturbations
- **step-parameter-expansion** [claim](#stmt-nl-parameter-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nl-parameter-expansion): Taylor expansion of the physical datum in the symmetry parameters -> invertible linear leading term with small remainder
- **step-blowup-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-blowup) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nl-parameter-expansion-2): Brouwer degree on the parameter ball placing data on the stable graph -> open set of blowup data
- **step-gaussian-corollary** [claim](#stmt-cor-gaussian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nl-parameter-expansion-3): Full support of the Gaussian law charges every nonempty open set -> positive probability of blowup

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-open-blowup-set**: There is a nonempty open set of high-regularity data on the twelve-dimensional torus whose solutions blow up in finite time at the self-similar rate.
- **inv-matching-degree**: The free matching quotient has Brouwer degree minus one on the parameter disk, so it has a zero there.
- **inv-gaussian-positive-probability**: Gaussian Fourier data with fast enough decay lie in the Sobolev space almost surely and blow up with positive probability.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-low-dimension**: Apply the blowup conclusion to low-dimensional physical wave models. -> The construction is carried out only in dimension twelve with a small scaling parameter; nothing is claimed elsewhere.
- **anti-small-power**: Use the theorem for any odd power or any regularity index. -> The power must be chosen sufficiently large first and the index is fixed only afterwards.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The main theorems are analytic, but the appendix contains finite exact rational checks. This panel executes one such check: the exact discriminant and its sign for the two quadratic factors of the high angular polynomial comparison.

```python
# INPUTS: one line with three decimal or integer strings a b c, the coefficients of a x^2 + b x + c.
# PRECONDITIONS: the three tokens parse as exact decimals.
# OUTPUTS: one line holding the exact discriminant as a reduced fraction followed by NEG, ZERO or POS.
# TIME: constant number of exact rational operations.
# SPACE: constant.
# ARITHMETIC: exact rational arithmetic with Fraction, no floating point.
# FAILURE: a malformed token raises an exception and the run is a candidate crash.
import sys
from fractions import Fraction
a, b, c = [Fraction(t) for t in sys.stdin.read().split()]
d = b * b - 4 * a * c
s = "NEG" if d < 0 else ("ZERO" if d == 0 else "POS")
print(str(d) + " " + s)
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves existence of blowup data and spectral stability, so it gives no program or library interface to implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns a twelve-dimensional torus, which no interactive or physical simulation uses, and it supplies no stepping scheme. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The appendix describes finite checks with integers and rationals: coefficient enclosures, sign tables and determinant bounds. A verifier can reproduce them without floating-point decisions. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main theorems are existence results obtained by contraction and degree arguments, with no stated procedure that returns blowup data. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No container, index or layout is defined; the objects are function spaces and spectral coordinates. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No dataflow, kernel or precision regime appears; the only finite computation is exact arithmetic for certificates. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, adversary or protocol appears in this analysis of a dispersive equation. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The random Gaussian datum is used only to show that its law charges an open set of blowup data, not for learning or generalisation. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No objective, constraint set or optimality claim appears; parameters are matched by topological degree, not optimised. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communicating processes, consensus or fault model appears. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The remaining relevance is to analysis of nonlinear dispersive equations, which is the paper's own subject and not a separate engineering domain. |
